## Document 020: 2018年末的剧本：美联储持续加息，美股从9月高点暴跌20%，迫使美联储1月“转向”

Source PDF: all report pdf/2018年末的剧本：美联储持续加息，美股从9月高点暴跌20%，迫使美联储1月“转向”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-21
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛交易主管认为，当前美股市场正重现 2018 年末货币紧缩与股市承压的局⾯。⾼盛⾦融
条件指数两周内收紧约 8 点，创⼀年新⾼，市场抛售也从科技股扩散⾄防御性板块。⻩⾦与
股票同步下跌，或反映投资者正抛售资产筹集现⾦，⻩⾦也因此成为观察流动性压⼒的关
键指标。
摘要：
美股持续下跌，⾦融条件急剧收紧，市场正显现出与 2018 年末相似的危险信号：美联储在市场
压⼒不断累积之际，仍⾯临继续加息的政策预期，投资者开始担忧货币政策是否会再次加剧市
场动荡。
据⾼盛交易主管 Rich Privorotsky 周五报告，当前市场与 2018 年末存在惊⼈的相似之处。他将近
期抛售定性为 “ 全⾯清仓式变现 ” ，⽽⾮普通的板块轮动，并警告市场可能正在从⻛险偏好下降转
向更深层次的流动性收缩。
彼时，美联储在 2018 年连续加息，标普 500 指数从 9 ⽉⾼点⾄ 12 ⽉ 24 ⽇低点累计下跌 19.8% ，逼
近技术性熊市。随着市场压⼒持续加剧，美联储最终在 2019 年 1 ⽉释放暂停加息的信号，政策⽴

场发⽣转变。
如今， Privorotsky 尤其关注⻩⾦⾛势。股票与⻩⾦同步遭到抛售，传统避险资产未能发挥保护
作⽤。在他看来，⻩⾦可能是判断当前流动性危机深度的最佳观察指标，其⾛势或将揭⽰市场
压⼒是否正在进⼀步恶化。
2018 年末的教训：持续加息最终迫使美联储转向
2018 年，美联储全年四次加息。 9 ⽉ 20 ⽇，标普 500 指数创下阶段⾼点，此后随着全球经济增⻓
放缓、贸易摩擦加剧以及货币政策持续收紧，市场压⼒不断累积。
12 ⽉，美联储再次加息，将联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间提⾼⾄ 2.25%—2.50% 。然⽽，市场对经济前
景和货币政策的担忧持续升温，标普 500 从 9 ⽉⾼点⾄ 12 ⽉ 24 ⽇低点累计下跌近 20% 。
市场抛售最终促使美联储调整政策⽴场。2019 年 1 ⽉ 30 ⽇，美联储表⽰未来调整利率时将保持耐
⼼，释放出暂停加息的明确信号，随后市场迎来反弹。
Privorotsky 认为，当前市场同样⾯临⾦融条件收紧与增⻓前景承压的双重压⼒，政策与市场之
间的⽭盾正在加深。

⾦融条件骤然收紧，市场从轮动转向全⾯抛售
Privorotsky 指出，当前市场最明显的变化，是抛售从局部板块扩散⾄整个市场。周四，标普 500
盘中⼀度上涨逾 1% ，最终却收跌 2% ，连续六个交易⽇下跌并再次跌破 200 ⽇均线。当天全部 11
个板块悉数收跌，公⽤事业、必需消费品等传统防御板块也未能幸免。
与此同时，⾼盛美国⾦融条件指数（ FCI ）宏观篮⼦周四升⾄ 124.48 ，创过去⼀年最⾼⽔平。⾃
11 ⽉初以来，该指数在约两周内上升约 8 个点，显⽰⾦融条件正在迅速收紧。
当前美联储官员接连释放鹰派信号，理事 Waller 关于加息 50 个基点的表态，以及会议纪要中官员
对继续加息的⽀持，进⼀步加剧了市场担忧。资本成本上升也令 AI 基础设施投资的回报预期承

### Page 2

压。
Privorotsky 认为，市场技术⾯已经遭到严重破坏，期权市场的负 Gamma 效应还在放⼤跌势。此
前的板块轮动，正在演变为更⼴泛的股票清仓。
⻩⾦成为流动性危机的最佳观察指标
在所有资产中， Privorotsky 尤其关注⻩⾦。他认为，⻩⾦是观察当前市场流动性状况最有价值
的同步指标。
通常情况下，⻩⾦被视为避险资产，但此次却与股票同步下跌。⾦价跌破 3600 美元，铜价也出
现单⽇约 5% 的⼤幅下挫。
这种跨资产同步下跌释放出不同于普通市场调整的信号：投资者不再只是从股票转向⻩⾦等避
险资产，⽽是在出售不同类别的资产以获取现⾦。市场⾯临的可能是更⼴泛的流动性收缩。
ANZ 指出，⻩⾦投机性多头仍有约 200 吨的潜在减仓空间，意味着去杠杆压⼒可能尚未完全释
放。
与此同时，⻓端国债收益率下⾏、通胀盈亏平衡利率收缩，股票与⻩⾦却同步遭到抛售。
Privorotsky 认为，这⼀组合表明市场关注点正在从通胀转向增⻓与流动性问题，债券市场或许
已经开始为政策失误定价。
因此，⻩⾦能否⽌跌企稳，将是判断市场流动性压⼒是否缓解的重要观察窗⼝。

市场何时迎来政策转向？

Privorotsky 认为，当前悲观情绪和单边去杠杆的规模，暗⽰市场可能正在接近战术性低点。由
于基⾦仓位已经明显低配，⼀旦出现反弹催化剂，市场反弹幅度可能相当剧烈。
但真正的转机仍取决于政策预期是否发⽣变化。他继续看好做多短期限国债，认为这⼀交易与
股票市场所反映的⻛险信号存在明显错位。
近期市场将关注 PCE 数据、⾮农就业报告和 12 ⽉ FOMC 会议。 Privorotsky 认为，真正可能迫使
美联储重新评估政策路径的是 11 ⽉ CPI 数据，但这⼀数据距离当前仍有⼀段时间。
值得注意的是，⾼盛经济学家⽬前仍预计美联储将在 12 ⽉加息 25 个基点，并于 2027 年 3 ⽉再次
加息，⽽债券市场定价与这⼀预测存在明显分歧。

价格走势分化，PPI涨幅扩大
——2026年 10月物价数据点评
[Table_Industry]日期: shzqdatemark [Table_Summary] 主要观点
价格走势分化，PPI 涨幅扩大
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，而上涨项
仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月
扩大 5.8个百分点，影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.63个百分点，带动能源价
格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除食品和能源价格的核
心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，小幅回升，基本保持稳定。 PPI 同比上涨
5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大 0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭
开采、石油开采、石油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金
属矿采选、有色金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9个行业合计影响 PPI 同
比上涨约 6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。

1 事件：10月 CPI、PPI数据公布
国家统计局数据显示，2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同
比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格
下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨 0.8%，服务
价格上涨 0.7%。1—10月平均，全国居民消费价格比上年同期上
涨 0.9%。10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，
农村下降 0.1%；食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消
费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格上涨 0.1%。
2026年 10月份，全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.7%，
环比上涨 0.7%。工业生产者购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，环比上涨
1.1%。1—10月平均，工业生产者出厂价格比上年同期上涨 2.7%，
工业生产者购进价格上涨 4.0%。
表 1：10月 CPI、PPI同比数据（%）
资料来源：IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因

2.1 CPI小幅回落
本月全国 CPI环比由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平。食品价格由上
月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%，影响 CPI环比下降约 0.17个百分点，
是 CPI 环比下降的主要原因。其中，猪肉、鲜菜、鸡蛋、鲜果、
水产品价格均有不同程度的下降。非食品方面，受国际市场价格
变动影响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.7 个百分
点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14个百分点；服务方面，服务价格由
上月下降 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04个百分
点，其中受国庆长假的刺激，相关旅游出行等服务价格有所上涨。
CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2 个百分点。具体来
看，食品价格下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响
CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点。食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格
分别下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54
个百分点。非食品方面，受汽油涨价影响，能源价格涨幅扩大。

汽油价格上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI

涨 36.6%，涨幅扩大 1.3个百分点；黑色金属矿采选业下降 6.4%，
跌幅扩大 3个百分点；石油和天然气开采业上涨 35%，涨幅扩大
10.5 个百分点；石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业上涨 29.9%，涨幅
扩大 8.3 个百分点；黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业上涨 0.4%，由负
转正。
3 事件影响：对经济和市场
3.1 价格走势分化，PPI涨幅扩大

宏观数据
请务必阅读尾页重要声明 8
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，
而上涨项仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除
食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2
个百分点，基本保持稳定。PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大
0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭开采、石油开采、石
油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金属矿采选、有色
金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约
6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。
4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 推动经济向新向优向好发展
10月价格持续分化，CPI走势偏稳，PPI持续上涨。CPI同比
的变动越来越受到非食品的影响，尤其是汽油价格，但由于食品
项偏弱的表现，CPI 整体平稳，核心 CPI 也保持稳定。工业品价

格同比涨幅有所扩大，上游原材料与下游制造业均有所涨价，尤
其是人工智能持续赋能产业转型带动计算机通信、电气机械等行
业上涨突出。政策方面，7月政治局会议提出要稳定生猪等农畜产
品生产和价格，实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政
策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量
政策，加大逆周期调节力度，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，
努力实现“十五五”良好开局。
5 风险提示：
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变
化。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：   
環境：鴻海採用 TCFD 框架為基礎，全面盤點與評估氣候相關風險與機會，
並導入 ISO 14001 環境管理系統與 ISO 45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，同時
也推動「低碳供應鏈轉型推動計畫」，協助超過 30 家供應商完成節能專案，
累計減少碳排放約 15,000 公噸 CO₂e。鴻海已有超過 150 家供應商完成碳盤
查並簽署科學基礎減碳目標（SBTi），提升能源使用效率與減碳成效。  
社會：鴻海秉持「以人為本」理念，長期支持教育與青年培訓，設立「三創
育成基金會」，提供新創團隊資金與資源支持，自 2015 年以來已經培育超
過  100 組科技創業團隊，促進創新創業生態系發展，為社會培訓科技人才。
治理：董事會有設立「永續發展委員會」，主管由具備三年以上公開發行公
司財務主管經歷之財務長兼任，該委員自 2023 年起每年召開至少四次會議，
全體委員及相關主管亦於任職期間完成 ESG 專業訓練課程，強化永續治理
能力與資訊揭露透明度。 
公司簡介：  
鴻海精密工業股份有限公司（Hon Hai Precision Industry），成立於 1974 年，
由郭台銘創辦，總部位於新北市土城區。公司早期以模具及電子零組件製造
起家，逐步擴展至電子產品組裝、系統整合及設計製造服務，現為全球最大
電子專業製造服務（EMS）廠商。 
集團業務涵蓋消費智能產品、雲端網路產品、電腦終端產品及元件與其他產
品四大領域，旗下包含工業富聯（FII）、鴻騰精密（FIT）等重要子公司，
並透過全球事業單位及子公司分工，提供零組件、模組至整機系統的垂直整
合服務。集團生產及服務據點遍及全球逾 20 個國家及地區，包括中國、台
灣、印度、越南、墨西哥及美國等，全球員工人數於季節性高峰約達 100 萬
人。 
鴻海主要競爭對手包括廣達、緯創、和碩、仁寶及立訊精密等，依產品領域
有所不同。近年積極布局 AI 伺服器、電動車及半導體等新興業務，其中 AI
伺服器受惠全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）資本支出擴張，成為重要成長動能，
並持續鞏固全球電子製造服務龍頭地位。 
3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元： 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，QoQ+19%，
YoY+46%，毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-0.1ppts，YoY-0.33ppts，主要受產品組合及
原物料上漲影響導致毛利率下滑；營業利益 1,154.99 億元，QoQ+22%，
YoY+64%，營益率 3.83%， QoQ+0.07ppts， YoY+0.4ppts，營業利益成長高
於營收及營業毛利，顯現隨營運規模擴大，生產進入規模經濟；歸屬母公司
淨利 775.24 億元，QoQ+29%， YoY+34%，稅後 EPS 5.51 元。營業利益、淨
利和 EPS 皆創下歷史單季新高。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.7%，預估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+11.7%：鴻海 10/2026 營
收 1.31 兆元，MoM+12.7%， YoY+45.7%，創單月歷史新高，除受惠 GB300
持續放量外，iPhone 新機推出亦帶動營收顯著增長；Vera Rubin 僅小量出
貨。展望 4Q26，我們預期 GB200/300 出貨量季增持平至微幅下滑，Vera 
Rubin 則有雙位數以上的成長，並且由於 Vera Rubin 的 ASP 近乎倍增，預
估伺服器營收將季增雙位數。消費電子方面，我們認為記憶體與處理器成本

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

3 群益投顧 
上升，將使手機出貨面臨壓力，但產品 ASP 提升及 Apple 穩健的品牌效應
仍將為出貨量提供支撐，因此 4Q26 仍將受惠新機推出與傳統旺季帶來的成
長。相較於消費電子的季節性，GB200/300 放量與產品組合變化對全年營運
的影響更大，因此 2026 年的主要成長引擎仍是伺服器業務的擴張；Vera 
Rubin 則為 2027 年的成長建立基礎。綜上所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 3.37
兆元，QoQ+11.7%；毛利率/營益率持續受 Vera Rubin 新平台出貨而稀釋，
下滑 0.10ppts/0.06ppts 來到 5.92%/3.77%，歸屬母公司淨利 899.02 億元，
QoQ+15.97%，稅後 EPS 6.39 元。 
雲端網路成為營運主軸，規模經濟支持本業獲利成長： 3Q26 雲端網路產品
占營收 54%，高於 2Q26 的 51%。消費智能、電腦終端及元件與其他產品分
別占 29%、12%及 5%。雲端網路產品受 AI 伺服器及機櫃出貨帶動，季增與
年增均強勁。消費智能產品亦受新品與傳統旺季帶動，季增優於公司原先預
期。據我們所知，伺服器約占雲端網路營收的 85%-90%，其中約 70%來自
AI 伺服器。隨雲端網路占比提高，AI 機櫃的交付時程、平台組合及物料認
列方式，將更直接影響鴻海的營收與獲利率。鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-
0.1ppts，主要受到整櫃 GPU 伺服器高昂的 BOM 所 稀 釋；營 業 利 益 率 3.83%，
QoQ+0.07ppts，雖然毛利率季減，營益率卻有所改善，反映出營收規模擴大
及營運效率提升的效益。 
Vera Rubin 接力 GB300，2027 年進入新平台主要貢獻期：AI 機櫃的新舊平
台交替是鴻海延續成長的核心動能。鴻海預計 2026 年 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨
較 2025 年倍增以上，且既有 GB 平台需求可能延續至 2027 年。此外，Vera 
Rubin 與 GB 系列同為 NVL72，因此我們預期鴻海將可較平滑的實現世代交
替。管理層預估 4Q26 Vera Rubin出貨量將呈高雙位數增長。展望 2027 年，
伺服器業務上會新增更多 Neocloud 客戶，但主要客戶仍然是大型 CSP。整
體訂單並未隨近期市場的擔憂而調整，對於 AI 投資的展望維持正向，預估
AI 機櫃出貨量將呈高雙位數成長。 
自製零組件與自動化支撐獲利，高 BOM 仍主導毛利率走勢：鴻海透過垂直
整合提高單一專案可承接的製造內容，管理層表示除規模經濟外，零組件自
製率的提高與自動化生產都將為毛利率及營益率提供支撐。根據我們調查，
在晶片以外的零組件自製率約 50%，布局包括連接器、線材、Midplane、交
換器及散熱相關產品，零組件毛利率高於 15%，部分甚至可達 20%，管理層
亦提出 800G 以上交換器全年營收倍增的展望，這些產品有助鴻海由機櫃組
裝向高速互聯及關鍵元件延伸，並可提升附加價值。然而據我們了解目前鴻
海在 GPU 的大型專案中尚未採用 consignment model，記憶體等元件漲價則
主要透過 cost-plus 模式轉嫁給客戶，進入 Vera Rubin 世代後，由於 ASP 將
近倍增，會進一步造成毛利率稀釋，考慮到 GPU Rack 單價極高，我們預估
2027 年毛利率仍將承壓。 
ASIC 拓展產品組合，物料模式與 ASP 差異使營收比重低於出貨量佔比：據
我們了解，鴻海 ASIC 平台約佔 AI 伺服器營收的 20%，較 1H26 略升，且
ASIC 平台成長速度高於 GPU 平台，除既有客戶外亦有新專案，管理層預估
3Q26 ASIC 伺服器出貨量 YoY+150%，高於 GPU 伺服器 70%的出貨量年增。
我們認為 ASIC 專案增加可擴大鴻海在不同運算平台的參與，減少成長來源
集中於單一 GPU 平台的程度。此外，大部分 ASIC 專案將有望採用
consignment model，有助於緩解 AI 伺服器對於整體毛利率的負面效果，然
而 GPU 平台 ASP 顯著高於 ASIC 平台，因此 ASIC 的營收較難完整反映其

提升亦有助提升營運效率，為毛利率及營益率提供部分支撐。考量上述原
因，我們給予鴻海 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 121: 261112_daiwa_eink

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_daiwa_eink.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12

### Page 1

Summary: E Ink held an investor call today, 12 November, after 3Q26 results were 
announced on 6 November (see our memo). Overall, its 3Q26 gross margin of 52.2% (vs. 
58.7% in 2Q26 and 56.0% in 3Q25) missed our estimate of 59.0% and the consensus 
estimate of 58.2%, mainly due to less revenue scale and unfavourable product mix within 
consumer electronics segment (ie, more module, less film). E Ink also talked about their 
cooperation with Taiflex (8039 TT, not rated) on PTFE material and management is 
optimistic towards the development progress.

We have a Hold (3) rating on E Ink with a 12-month TP of TWD165, based on a target PER 
of 14x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. For more information on the company, 
please refer to our latest flash, Downgrading: weaker-than-expected order visibility, on 13 
August 2026.

What’s the impact  
• 3Q26 results recap. E Ink’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD9,936m (-2.7% QoQ, -4.6% YoY) 
was roughly in line with our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates. Its 3Q26 gross 
margin of 52.2% (vs. 58.7% in 2Q26 and 56.0% in 3Q25) missed our estimate of 
59.0% and the consensus estimate of 58.2%, mainly due to less revenue scale and 
unfavourable product mix within its consumer electronics segment (ie, more module, 
less film). Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD2,652m (-29.0% QoQ, -37.3% YoY) beat our and 
consensus estimates by 21.6% and 15.2%, respectively. Its 3Q26 EPS came in at 
TWD2.29. Note E Ink’s 3Q26 product mix was: consumer electronics 54.6% (-9.4% 
YoY), IoT (including ESL and large signage) 45.4% (+1.8% YoY). 
• 2026 and 2027 guidance. E Ink slightly revised down its 2026 full-year guidance: total 
revenue to grow by 9% YoY (down from previous 10-15%); consumer electronics to 
decline by 15% YoY, negatively affected by the memory price hikes; IoT to grow by 
35%+ YoY; and signage to account for high-single-digit % of total revenue. For 2027, 
the company is still positive on the growth of the IoT segment, but consumer the 
consumer electronics segment will depend on how the new generations of end 
products perform. 
• Cooperation with Taiflex (8039 TT, not rated) on PTFE. E Ink has announced it will 
work with Taiflex to co-develop the next generation of CCL material – PTFE. The two 
companies will combine Taiflex’s expertise in CCL and high-end electronics materials, 
and E Ink’s capabilities in mass production, surface treatment and roll-to-roll 
processing. E Ink expects to see preliminary results by 2027 and significant revenue 
contribution from 2028. 
• Capacity expansion and future trends. E Ink’s H5 plant is performing on track and 
its utilisation rate and yield rate are improving. The H6 plant has also started to install 
equipment in November 2026, and is scheduled for a trial run in 2027 and contribution 
to revenue in 2028. The company expects both plants to have capability to 
manufacture large signage. Combining the trend that E Ink’s customers and the 
retailers are now planning to implement more larger signages (mid-sized, 10-15 
inches, vs. 2 inches for ESL) as retail advertisement, the company is confident that 
signage will replace ESL to become the fastest growing segment.

What we recommend 
We have a Hold (3) rating on E Ink with a 12-month TP of TWD165, based on a target PER 
of 14x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Based on our 2026/27E EPS, the stock 
is currently trading at PERs of 12.3x/11.3x, vs. its past-3-year trading range of 11-32x. Key 
upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected ESL demand, 2) stronger-
/weaker-than-expected eReader sales on macroeconomic headwinds, and 3) progress on 
capacity expansion being early/delayed.

## Document 155: 261117_ubs_aspeed-DG

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
ASPEED Technology Inc
Near-term BMC momentum peaking;
downgrade to Neutral
Three reasons for our downgrade to Neutral
We remain constructive about Aspeed’s growth potential and competitive positioning
longer term, but are downgrading the stock from Buy to Neutral on limited upside vs
expectation in 2027-28. Key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied
to overall Cloud AI TAM expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US

mid-terms and constraint of power / datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that
Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market server units in 2026, leading to
uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases / demand slows; 3) we
see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
Possible order moderation in 2027, after some over-build this year
Aspeed's BMC units grew 78% YoY to reach ~34mn in 2026, of which we estimate

9.5mn units for accelerator servers, and 23.5mn units for general purpose servers. Our
analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds
the end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at
hyperscalers which could cap the upside into 2027. We note that healthy shipment ratio
of Aspeed's BMC units vs end market should be ~80-90% considering Aspeed's 80%
market share, with 5-10% upside for switch, power supply etc. The ratio likely rises to

104% in 2026 from 70%+ in 2024-25. See Figure 1-3 for our analysis.   We lower 2027
BMC units from 48.2mn to 42.4mn, assuming the BMC shipment to reach near term
peak of 11-12m/Q in Q426-Q127, and to soften moderately to ~10m/Q in H227. We
still consider robust growth of AI accelerater BMCs at 82% in 2027E vs 76% in 2026E.
Product roadmap reinforces long-term leadership
Longer term, we remain constructive on Aspeed’s strong execution, accelerated product

roadmap, and rising silicon content per server. Aspeed remains highly focused on BMC
development, unlike  many competitors where BMC is one of several priorities. Its
roadmap, including more advanced BMCs such as the 6nm AST2800, enhanced security
features, and adjacent products such as SMC, I/O expanders and FPGA integration,
should reinforce its leading position in the Cloud AI market over the next 3-5 years. See
our view on Marvell’s infrastructure management controller (IMC) introduction.

Valuation: D/G to Neutral from Buy; lower target to NT$10,000 from NT$19,000
We trim 2027/28E EPS by 14%/11%. Our new estimates are 10/12% below VA
consensus. Aspeed's order outlook remains robust for 2027, but we see risk of
moderation in coming quarters. Downgrade to Neutral from Buy. Our new target of NT
$10,000 is based on 30x 2027E PE, as we roll back from 43x 2027-28E PE given the
rising uncertainty. 30x PE is below Aspeed's historical mid-cycle of 36x, but we view

reasonable considering the potential demand slowdown and 30% LT earnings CAGR.

### Page 2

ASPEED Technology Inc UBS Research
Thesis MapUBS Research THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Can BMC units continue to grow in the next two years after rising 78% in 2026E?
Although we remain constructive about Aspeed's long term execution and technology leadership,
we see a possible order moderation into 2027 after over-build in 2026E. We estimate that BMC
shipment will rise 78% YoY in 2026E and reach around 34m total BMC shipment units with 23.5m

dedicated for general purpose servers—this suggests potential pre-building of BMC inventory by
hyperscalers, as end market general purpose server units are only at 22.7m units in 2026E. In
addition, we are also monitoring the risk of AI data center buildout slowdown in 2028 post the recent
US elections and power constraints. We now forecast BMC shipment growth to moderate in
2027E/28E, with 25%/13% YoY unit shipment growth respectively.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain its dominant 70%-plus market share in server BMCs?

Yes. We expect Aspeed to maintain its market share in the next 3-4 years due to 1) We expect
migration to AST2700 to accelerate in 2027 from a mild 2026 ramp up, with new Intel and AMD CPU
platforms as well as key cloud ASIC projects and Nvidia's new Rubin Ultra AI GPU platform in 2027; 2)
Aspeed has enhanced its BMC offering by embedding flexibility to satisfy customer requirements; 3)
Aspeed is expanding its product portfolio to I/O expanders that integrate functionalities built on

FPGA, RoT security chips, and mini-BMCs (SMCs). 4) Aggressive business strategy to build inventory
for hyperscalers since 2025, which we believe should limit opportunity for competitors.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain overall gross margins at a high-60s level?
Yes. We expect Aspeed's GM to remain in the high-60s over the next few years. Drivers include: 1) the
roll-out of the higher-end AST2700 series, which could solidify Aspeed's competitive position and

help it sustain market share; 2) Aspeed managing back-end logistics for AST2700, reducing reliance
on GUC; and 3) Aspeed raising prices (effective Dec 1) for BMCs to pass through cost inflation.
We are constructive for Aspeed's long-term competitive positioning but are downgrading it to
Neutral. Our key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied to overall Cloud AI TAM
expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US mid-terms and constraint of power /

datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market
server units in 2026, leading to uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases /
demand slows; 3) we see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
1) Our analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds the
end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at hyperscalers which

could cap the upside into 2027. 2) The outcome of recent US mid-terms creates higher uncertainty
for new AI data center build-out in 2028.
Aspeed's share price has rallied ~65% YTD (vs Taipei Exchange Index's 11% gain), but declined 54%
from its peak in Sep. The stock is now trading at 29x/22x 2027/28E PE, de-rated from its peak of
around 63x/48x in Sept, suggesting the market is taking a more conservative stance regarding overall
Cloud AI outlook and Aspeed's growth.

Company Description
ASPEED is the world's largest baseboard management controller (BMC) chipset provider.
ASPEED introduced the first generation of server management processors in 2005. It acquired
Emulex Pilot's remote server management chip business in 2016. Besides server management

chipsets, ASPEED also produces PC/AV extension solutions and image processing system-on-
chips (SoCs). In 2023, server management chipsets accounted for 88% of total sales.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We derive our price target for Aspeed from PE valuation methodology relative to its historical
trading range.
We believe key upside risks for Aspeed include: 1) stronger-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers and from AI servers with less crowding out of traditional

servers; 2) further market share expansion; 3) faster-than-expected server platform upgrades;
4) faster-than-expected progress in expanding the addressable content within servers.
We believe key downside risks for Aspeed include: 1) weaker-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers; 2) market share losses or more intense industry competition; 3)
slower-than-expected server platform upgrades; 4) slower-than-expected progress in
expanding the addressable content within servers.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 238: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

표 5. 삼성전자 이론적 적정 주가는 465,690원 
투자 지표 내용 비고

당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% YoY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% YoY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% YoY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

## Document 284: Anthropic世纪IPO，创始人套现了130亿！

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic世纪IPO，创始人套现了130亿！.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 以创纪录 IPO 募资逾千亿美元，七位联合创始⼈合计套现近 20 亿美元（约合⼈⺠
币 133.6 亿元），⾕歌、亚⻢逊等科技巨头合计持股约 28% 。然⽽， IPO 融资难以覆盖其庞
⼤的资本开⽀：公司已披露的基础设施承诺⾼达 1.05 万亿⾄ 1.08 万亿美元，未来仍将⾼度
依赖外部融资， AI 巨头的烧钱压⼒正浮出⽔⾯。
摘要：
Anthropic 以⼀场史⽆前例的 IPO 正式登陆公开市场，刷新科技公司上市融资纪录，同时也揭⽰
出这家 AI 巨头未来⾯临的天⽂数字级资本需求。
本次 IPO 定价每股 83 美元，⾼于此前 74 美元的区间中值， Anthropic 通过发⾏ A 类普通股及特殊 N
类股合计募资约 1048 亿美元，若承销商⾏使全部超额配售权，总额可达约 1170 亿美元。这⼀规
模远超 Facebook 2012 年 IPO 时创下的 160 亿美元纪录。
七位联合创始⼈在此次 IPO 中各⾃出售约 340 万股，按 83 美元定价计算，每⼈税前套现约 2.8 亿美

元，七⼈合计套现近 20 亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 133.6 亿元）。这⼀数字超出创始⼈此前按 74 美元中
值价格预设的约 2.5 亿美元⽬标，每⼈额外进账约 3000 万美元。
创始⼈套现逾 20 亿美元，慈善承诺存弹性空间
七位创始⼈的套现规模引发市场关注。 Anthropic 在 IPO ⽂件中披露，每位创始⼈已承诺将其个
⼈持股的 80% ⽤于慈善事业，以此回应外界对创始⼈⼤规模变现的质疑。
然⽽，该承诺的约束⼒存在明显局限。公司在⽂件中明确指出，上述承诺属于 " ⾃愿、可撤销的
慈善意向声明，不构成具有法律约束⼒的义务 " ，且若创始⼈认为有必要，可缩减慈善捐赠⽐
例。
科技巨头争相⼊股，亚⻢逊与⾕歌合计持股 28%
除公开发⾏的 A 类普通股外， Anthropic 还向多家科技巨头定向发售了总计 260 亿美元的 N 类⽆投
票权股份，吸引了⾏业内最具影响⼒的战略投资者。
其中，英伟达与⾕歌各认购 100 亿美元 N 类股份， AMD 、博通、美光、微软及台积电⽠分剩余份
额。

在整体股权结构上，⾕歌持有 Anthropic 约 13.4% 的股份；亚⻢逊则通过将此前投资时获得的可
转换票据部分转股，持股⽐例达 14.6%—— 若亚⻢逊将全部票据转换为股权，持股⽐例最⾼可接
近 24% 。两⼤云计算巨头合计持有 Anthropic 约 28% 的股份。
逾万亿美元基础设施承诺，资本需求远超融资规模
尽管本次 IPO 融资规模创下历史纪录， Anthropic ⾯临的资本压⼒依然巨⼤。
根据截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的季度数据， Anthropic 未来 12 个⽉内仅算⼒与基础设施⽀出承诺就⾼
达 707 亿美元；⾄ 2030 年底前，这⼀数字将攀升⾄ 4516 亿美元。此外，公司还积累了数千亿美
元的其他或有承诺及尚未定价的⽀出项⽬。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

得及此前账⾯资⾦，公司可⽤营运资本约达 2300 亿美元，但与其庞⼤的承诺规模相⽐，缺⼝依
然悬殊。这意味着 Anthropic 在可预⻅的未来仍将持续依赖外部融资，其资本消耗速度将是投资

## Document 307: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.
Exhibit 1: Economy Rebounding From Tariff Shock But Growth Likely To Remain Moderate, In Line With Labor Supply And
Robust Productivity Underpinnings.
A) Growth led by manufacturing revival but surveys overall still consistent with moderate

economic conditions.
B) Easing economic policy uncertainty from its April 2025 tariff-related peak has renewed
business demand for labor, and labor supply responded in kind.

4 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

Investment Implications
We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and
broaden portfolio exposure. As
earnings growth moderates and
market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.

Investment Implications
With China’s growth model still favoring
exports over domestic consumption,
resource protectionism and an
emphasis on domestic investment not
just in the U.S. but around the globe are

likely to continue to be themes in 2027.
We remain overweight the U.S., as its
well-diversified services-led economy
should be relatively less exposed to
China’s manufactured goods exports.
Still, the impact of competition from
China on U.S. technology supremacy is
an ongoing key risk to watch.

Chicago Fed National Activity Index is a monthly index designed to gauge overall economic activity and related inflationary pressure.

## Document 309: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

## Document 316: Consumer sentiment_ gloomy lead-in to festive season (2026-11-23)

Consumer sentiment: gloomy lead-in to festive season
By Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting, Westpac
13:54 November 10 2026 Share
A short video update from the Westpac Economics team.
The consumer mood remains unambiguously gloomy, growing concerns about the near term outlook
for the economy and jobs adding to intense pressure on family finances and an expected further rise in

interest rates. Reported plans for Christmas spending plans were not quite so weak but the wider
sentiment picture leading into the festive high season is bleak.

## Document 339: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 395: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 409: The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: The Big Picture: Plunge Protection
Date:    6 hours ago
The Big Picture: Plunge Protection

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
The Big Picture: Plunge
Protection
By Ed Yardeni • 23 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ma Fo / Unsplash

I. Finding Support
The S&P 500 may be finding support around 7,000, which marked the
index's record high earlier this year, just before the Middle East war
started (chart). If so, the S&P 500 may have bottomed on Friday at
6,910.39. Yet another V-shaped rebound is also possible. Nevertheless,
we did lower our year-end target for the second month in a row on
Saturday to 7,500 from 7,900. We are still using $425 per share as our
2027 forward earnings estimate at year-end 2026. But we are lowering our

### Page 2

forward P/E estimate from 18.6 to 17.6 to reflect ongoing jitters about the
AI trade.
We still view the post-midterms selloff as a buying opportunity. We
currently project the S&P 500 will rise to 8,500 to 9,000 by the end of
2027.
We should mention that if support doesn't hold at 7,000, the next level is
the low of 6,343.72 on March 30 of this year.
The Nasdaq low on March 30, 2025 was 20,794.64.
II. Historical Perspective

### Page 3

We view the recent S&P 500 selloff as another correction in the bull
market that started on October 12, 2022 (chart).
The S&P 500 remains in a significant uptrend that began in March 2009,
marking the trough of the bear market of the Great Financial Crisis (chart).
The same holds for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 4

The Nasdaq has been outperforming the S&P 500 since the late 1970s
(chart).
III. Earnings
Analysts' consensus earnings estimates have been rising significantly for
2026, 2027, and 2028 since the start of this year (chart). This is a very
unusual trifecta. The previous trifecta was more muted in 2024, 2025, and
2026. The question is whether analysts' current projections are irrationally
exuberant. They might be, but we don't expect significant downward

revisions like those during the Tech Wreck of the early 2000s. Importantly,
we also don't expect a recession over the rest of the Roaring 2020s.

### Page 5

The strong upward revisions in analysts' earnings estimates for 2026-2028
are pushing S&P 500 forward earnings to record highs. That's likely to
continue if the rest of the decade remains recession-free. However, the
recent ascent in forward earnings is likely to slow, especially if industry
analysts cut their outlook for the volatile S&P 500 Semiconductor industry.
The y/y growth rate of S&P 500 forward earnings is highly correlated with
the comparable growth rate in nominal GDP (chart). The widespread

notion that the stock market is not the economy is wrong. The economy
obviously drives corporate earnings. Both are performing well.

### Page 6

IV. 2026 Versus Past 10 Years
For the S&P 500, 2026 has been much more volatile than the past 10-
year average (chart).
The same can be said for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 7

V. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E is now lower than it was on Liberation Day 2025
(chart). It is well below the peaks of the Tech Wreck in the early 2000s.
We don't see much more downside barring a recession.
The forward P/E of the S&P 500 Growth composite has plunged since
mid-2025 (chart).

### Page 8

Growth is very cheap. The ratio of the forward P/Es of S&P 500 Growth
and Value is down to 1.03, the lowest since the bear market of the Great
Financial Crisis (chart).

Our Q&A explores where ING’s Global Outlook diﬀers from
market expectations, from the path for interest rates and
inﬂation to US debt, European ﬁscal risks, wages and gold

Carsten Brzeski, Michiel Tukker, Peter Vanden Houte, James
Knightley and Ewa Manthey
Our Q&A examines how ING’s Global Outlook diﬀers from market expectations
and where we see the biggest disconnects
What is the biggest disconnect today
between what the market is pricing and
ING’s Global Outlook?
Our base case scenario is more dovish and benign than markets
are currently pricing in. We expect oil prices to fall further, easing
inﬂationary pressures and allowing central banks to stop hiking
rates after a ﬁnal rate hike in December.
Due to weaker household balance sheets, we see only limited pass-
through from higher energy prices to core inﬂation, with companies
instead having to squeeze proﬁt margins. In this scenario, major

central banks could even start considering rate cuts towards the
end of 2027.
Is it normal for markets to be pricing in
virtually no rate cuts? Beyond 12 months,
interest rate expectations are virtually ﬂat.
How does that compare to historic rate hike
cycles?
Compared to today’s curve, in 2008 and early 2023 markets priced
in a much larger decline in rates beyond the near-term peak. The
current curve is much more similar in shape to the 2007 hiking

cycle.
The current curve is more similar to the
2007 hiking cycle than the one in 2023
Based on these prior hiking episodes, we foresee two conditions
that would warrant a more humped yield curve, i.e. a curve with a
lower terminal policy rate. The ﬁrst is a material slowdown in the
growth outlook. The most extreme version of this is the 2008 policy
error by the European Central Bank – we all know what happened
after the ECB decided to hike in 2008. So far, the growth outlook

remains robust, and we do not see this as a likely path for 2027.
The second trigger of a more humped-shaped yield curve would be
a decline in inﬂation risks. Once inﬂation peaked in 2023, markets

started repricing a lower neutral rate. In the near term, the curve still
held a hiking bias, but markets started pricing in a lower ECB rate
further out in the future. We believe this is the more likely path rates
will take and therefore expect a repricing lower by mid-2027.
Does Europe have a 'K-shaped' economy
like the US? And is there scope for
household savings to shield consumer
spending from higher energy prices in 2027?
Lower-income households generally suﬀer more from higher

energy prices than higher-income households, as they spend a
larger share of their income on energy and food. The energy shock
could therefore produce a more K-shaped consumption pattern.
However, Europe’s extensive social safety net provides important
protection for lower-income groups. Indeed, the consumer-
conﬁdence gap between the highest and lowest income quartiles
has not widened in 2026. Quite the opposite.
Since energy prices began rising, the savings rate has fallen,

cushioning the negative impact on consumption. However, with
households’ unemployment expectations increasing, it would be
overly optimistic to assume that the eurozone household savings
rate will continue to decline.
US growth has remained strong, but
deﬁcits are rising and a Democrat-run
Congress could put pressure on data centre
construction. Is the US heading towards a
debt crisis?
The US's ﬁscal position is poor. The government is currently
spending 4% of GDP on interest costs while running a primary

deﬁcit (tax revenue less departmental and programme spending) of
2.5% of GDP. The mid-term election results mean the primary deﬁcit
is pretty much locked in at 2.5% of GDP, with President Trump likely
vetoing any Democrat tax and spending policies approved in
Congress.

### Page 4

To keep the debt-to-GDP ratio stable, the economy needs to grow
faster than its debt pile to the tune of the primary deﬁcit. Today,
the US government is paying an average borrowing cost of 3.6%, so
nominal GDP needs to grow 6.1% per year to keep debt-to-GDP at
100% (6.1%-3.6%=2.5%). Amid rising government borrowing costs
and with nominal GDP growth closer to 5%, the debt-to-GDP ratio
looks set to climb.
But nothing is set in stone. For example, if AI leads to a

technological and productivity revolution that boosts growth and
tax revenue while lowering inﬂation and allowing interest costs to
fall, then the maths – and the outlook for the debt-to-GDP ratio –
changes dramatically.
Would the ECB lowering government
borrowing costs through bond purchases
encourage governments to borrow more,
worsening already-high debt levels? And is
it too soon for the ECB to ease policy, with
tools like QE?
Indeed, markets are inserting more disciplinary pressures on

governments that the European ﬁscal framework has failed to do.
This is why the ECB will be hesitant to restart bond purchases, at
least as long as governments are not subscribing to austerity
measures.
However, if the French ﬁscal crisis leads to contagion across the
eurozone while the rest of the eurozone sticks to the ﬁscal rules, the
ECB is likely to start its Transmission Protection Instrument to ring-
fence France and prevent contagion. The risk of starting any kind of

QE too early is indeed that it could create the wrong incentives for
ﬁscal policy.
The last US jobs report saw a small
uptick in wage growth. How likely is that to
accelerate, given tighter immigration policy

### Page 5

and labour shortages in some sectors, and
recent strength in the jobs data?
The best leading indicator for wage growth is the quits rate – the
proportion of workers quitting their jobs to move to a new position.
It peaked at 3% in 2022 as the post-Covid reopening fuelled intense
competition for workers. Companies responded by raising pay to
attract and retain staﬀ, pushing wage growth to 5.5%.
That situation has since reversed, with the quits rate down to 2%

and wage growth slowing sharply to just 3%. We are in a low-hire,
low-ﬁre economy with a lack of turnover in positions – few
companies are hiring, and few people are quitting, meaning
companies don't feel any real need to oﬀer big pay awards.
Some sectors are reporting shortages of skilled workers and there
will be pockets of strength, but until the quits rate moves
meaningfully higher, wage growth nationally will remain benign.
How will your outlook for the Federal

Reserve affect gold prices in 2027?
We expect the Fed to remain relatively cautious on rate cuts for
much of 2027, meaning monetary policy is unlikely to provide a
strong tailwind for gold early in the year. However, as expectations
for further easing build, lower yields and a potentially softer dollar
should become more supportive for bullion.
Combined with continued central bank buying, geopolitical
uncertainty and investor demand, we expect gold prices to be

supported in 2027, although periods of higher yields and dollar
strength could still trigger volatility.
